# STATUS -- where deepMoE is today

Local decode of DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (a 552B backbone + 196B of Engram, 16B activated in decode,
510 GB of weights) on one Strix Halo (Ryzen AI Max+ 395 / Radeon 8060S / 128 GB LPDDR5X / NVMe).
`main` is the Linux line; the Windows figures that appear below are labelled where they are.

This file is the **only entry point**. The design is in [design.md](design.md) (v0.9), but the design
says what *should* be; this says **what was measured, what was thrown away, and in what order to do
the next thing**. Before believing any number, look at the source to its right.
Anything with no source is marked `not measured`.

> The method comes from the same owner's `fleet-mi300x`: every optimisation is attributed by a
> decomposable per-token timeline; every experiment -- the ones kept and the ones backed out -- is
> recorded here with a number; the tests are audited by mutation injection;
> and every change that is kept has to pass two gates, "correctness" and "bit-for-bit determinism".

## 1. Today's numbers

**It speaks.** A `deepmoe serve` process keeps the pinned set, expert cache, and KV resident;
`tools/chat.py` starts a conversation and streams tokens, with each new turn prefilling only the added portion.
Correctness against the fp32 reference is 8/8 at each of the 64 token / 4K / 17K contexts.

### 1.0 Machine-recorded measurements (ledger)

The table below is **not handwritten**: when `tools/hitrate_bench.py` starts, it writes the commit, uncommitted files, `git diff` hash, exe / shader hashes,
all `DEEPMOE_*` switches, and GPU DPM state to `<run>/provenance.json` (`tools/provenance.py`);
`tools/perf_report.py --chat RUN --record LABEL` (with `--capture --record` for the hot step) appends numbers computed from the run's own files, together with their sources, to
`bench/results/perf_ledger.jsonl`; `tools/perf_ledger.py --write` renders this block again (`--check` checks whether it is stale).
**The main text interprets these rows without copying the numbers again**; cite them as "ledger #N".

`†` = this run predates `provenance.json`, so the code column is the tree and the binaries **as recorded**, not as run. ms/miss is the regression slope of a decode step's NVMe stall against that step's miss count, over the steps with hit > 0.8: a rerun of the same configuration moves it by only ~1%, which is far steadier than tok/s, so judge an IO change on it. start KV is the number of tokens `serve` restored from the on-disk KV cache at startup and replayed into the expert cache (the tail of the previous run's context; 0 = a clean start, which most rows older than STATUS §7 0s are not). disk writes is everything written to any NVMe during the run (the engine itself writes only a few MB of logs; >500 MB is marked ⚠, meaning another program was writing the same drive and the IO numbers cannot be trusted). bg GPU is the mean `gpu_busy_percent` over the second before the run started (another process holding the same LPDDR5X costs every kernel a few percent). What follows `dirty` is the hash of `git diff HEAD`; the full provenance -- exe and shader hashes, every switch, the DPM state -- is in that row of the ledger.

The following historical table is from the Windows period; each source is in the rightmost column.

| Metric | Today | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **Conversation decode** | **3.4–4.5 tok/s** (hit 0.84–0.90); per token ≈ 90–100 ms compute + **124–193 ms NVMe stall** | `design.md` §15.1.1 |
| Conversation decode (5500 slots / 96.34 GiB cache, 8-turn script) | **6.05 tok/s, hit 0.9431, stall 46–71 ms** | `p4_summary.md` §6 |
| **Hot step** (all experts resident, 64 token context) | **81.5 ms = 12.3 tok/s** (`--cache-gb 24`, entirely path A); 4K 91.1 ms; 17K 94.2 ms | `p2_decode.md` §10 |
| **Hot step, 2026-09-18 retest (Track PD)** | **102.0–106.2 ms**, 41 submits, identical with `--trace` on or off. Breakdown: attn **40** / moe gpu **38–40** / engram 4.9 / tail 6.0 / other 11. Per-dispatch trace: **busy 85.23 ms + gap 16.84 ms = span 102.07 ms**, of which **16.04 ms is the gate host round trip before each MoE layer**; barriers across 700 non-MoE dispatches total **0.80 ms** | This section §3 49; `bench/results/p4pd/trace_hot.bin` |
| **Hot step, 2026-09-19 (after Track K1a)** | **97.0 ms**, moe gpu **34.33** (`auto`, three ABAB pairs, A arm sd 2.1%). Entirely path A (`--cache-gb 24`) was **92.7 / 30.97** on the same day | §3 55; `plan_p5.md` §3(h) 9.2 |
| **Both directions of path placement policy lost (Track K1b)** | Hypothesis (§3 30: MoE **reads** slowly from path B) → place new experts on path A: **−5.4%** over four conversation turns. The reverse (place them on path B because **writes** to path A are expensive—Track Q2's 4 MiB `ReadFile` **704 µs vs 70 µs**): **+7.0%** over four turns (stall −6.78 ms), but **−20.5%** on the eight-turn topic-switching script (hit 0.914 → 0.879, fills 53,957 → 76,102, two ABAB pairs)—because it also froze path A's 3,400 slots into a first-touch pin. **Both NO-GO; the default remains one global LRU**. What remains is instrumentation (`hits_path_a/b`, `fills_path_a/b`, on by default) and two measurements: path-B read cost today **0.036 ms/expert-read**, versus path-A write cost **~6.8 ms/token** | §3 55; `plan_p5.md` §3(i); `bench/results/k1b/` |
| **Staged fill stopped at the bench (Track S1)** | Hypothesis: filling a P0 miss into path A incurs **704 µs/chunk** of page locking (Q2); replacing it with “read into a GPU-readable pinned host ring + one `vkCmdCopyBuffer` into path A” could save 5 ms/token of stall. The new `stage` mode in `io_dst_bench` (`--gpu-copy`, end-to-end wall time including the copy, n=5): QD 8 **4.7633 → 4.7689 GB/s (+0.12%)**, QD 24 **4.7040 → 4.7202 (+0.35%)**, against A-arm noise floors of **0.69% / 0.40%**—the criterion was ≥5%, **a factor of roughly thirty short; no runtime line was changed**. Mechanism: Q2's 8 submission threads **already** keep the 704 µs off the critical path—**`patha` and `pathb` have effectively identical queue depth at issue** (6.87/6.87, 22.02/22.04). This also supplies the number missing from Q2 §12.2: `vkCmdCopyBuffer` path B → path A for 18.8 MB = **0.145 ms (129 GB/s)**; submitting 8 regions together instead costs **0.201 each (93.5)**—**bandwidth-bound, not submission-bound**; folding it into the MoE command buffer buys nothing. Also, **`gpu::Device` creates only one compute queue**, so there is no second queue to hide the copy: 21.3 misses × 0.145 = **3.1 ms/token of queue occupancy** | §3 56; `p4_p0_queue.md` §16–§21; `bench/results/s1/` |
| **Decode had always run the M=6 MoE kernel (Track K1a)** | **The engine had never created an M=1 pipeline**: `moe_bridge.cpp` built the runner with `spec.m = kMoeBatchMax` (=6) so the same runner could handle verify batches; decode only set `live_columns` to 1. Thus each decode token used six accumulators to compute one column. `MoeRunner` now has an M=1 specialization (gate/up + h quantization + down must change together, since the fp8 h-plane offset depends on M): **moe gpu 37.87 → 34.33 (−9.3%, none of the six cells overlap), hot step 101.8 → 97.0 (−4.7%)**. The live-column mask from `fb53514` accounts for only 1.6 ms of that (microbench 0.6547 → 0.6028, −7.9%); **the M=6 shape accounts for another ~3.2** | `plan_p5.md` §3(h); `DEEPMOE_MOE_STATIC_M1=0` is the A arm |
| ✅ The **25%** gap between the two rows above is **explained** (Track G) | **It has four causes.** ① **Different commands**: Track I used `--cache-gb 24`; today's default `auto` = 5,100 slots, with **17 of 51 slabs on path B** → same binary, same session: `--cache-gb 24` **96.4 ms / moe gpu 33.8**, `--cache-gb 48` 96.6 / 33.8, `auto` **102.1 / 38.4**, **+5.5 ms** (the first engine-level manifestation of §3 30). ② **Track T's live-column mask** (`fb53514`, the only commit to change the MoE kernel after Track I): same-session `kernel_bench`, same raw read; `fb53514^` **0.6035 ms/pair** → HEAD **0.6426**, M=1 **+6.5% = +1.6 ms/token**; the same commit sped M=6 up 1.56×, **but M=6 belonged to the speculative batch already reverted in §3 41/42**. ③ Host cost of `vkQueueSubmit2`: with the same 41 submits, `submit` **1.6 → 4.2 ms/token = +2.6**. ④ The remaining **+7.2 ms** (attn 36.0 → 39.0, moe still 2.9 higher after subtracting kernel drift, engram +0.7, moe host +0.6) **remains unexplained**, on the same thread as limitation 6.5. Total **+16.9**, compared with 81.5 → 96.4 (`--cache-gb 24`) / 102.1 (`auto`). ⚠️ **2026-09-19 correction (Track K1a)**: ② is **4.8**, not 1.6—the mask is only a third of it; the other ~3.2 comes from “decode had always run the M=6 kernel”; hence ① is **2.90 ms**, not 5.5 (same-day retest). After K1a, `auto` is **97.0 / 34.33**, and `--cache-gb 24` is **92.7 / 30.97** | `plan_p5.md` §3(g) 8.7 + §3(h)/(i); `bench/results/p4g/ab_summary.csv` |
| **Second read source implemented (Track D2)** | A byte-for-byte copy of the same checkpoint on a USB SSD (E:), enabled by `--mirror DIR` / `DEEPMOE_MODEL_MIRRORS`, **off by default**. Routing uses **weighted least in-flight bytes** (weights from a startup random-read probe of 1 s / 4 MiB / QD 8), routing only P0 and P3. Copy: **510 GB / 529 s = 964 MB/s**; all 94 file lengths and all 5 sampled SHA-256 hashes matched. Aggregate random reads (same 6.8 GB shard, 4 MiB, QD 16): D: alone **3.453** → D:+E: **4.555 GB/s (+31%)**, split **78 : 22**, **with no loss in D:'s share** (3.53 vs 3.47). ⚠️ **End-to-end measurement still failed; both attempts failed on the same drive** (§3 58 and **59**). **2026-09-19 afternoon retest (Track D3)**: after physically reconnecting E:, `ls` / `nvme_bench` (**0.962 GB/s**) **both passed**, but the engine with `--mirror` still failed during initialization—`pinned load of 'norm.weight': overlapped read failed (**win32 1117 = ERROR_IO_DEVICE**)`, **on the same tensor as in the morning**. **There is no A/B table because the B arm could not start**; today's off arm was **4.5693 tok/s / stall 113.5 ms / hit 0.8957** (5,000 slots). **Recovery of light I/O ≠ a usable drive** | §3 58 / **59**; `p4_dual_source.md` §4 / **§7** |
| **Second read source: the third measurement failed too (Track D4)** | **The DX acceptance gate PASSED; the engine still failed.** After changing E:'s USB port and running `chkdsk /spotfix`, `dx_probe` with 48 handles × QD 24 sustained **1.04 GB/s with zero errors in each of two 300-second arms**, and handle overhead fell **17 s → 158 ms**; the engine's first `--mirror` cell still failed with **win32 1117 / `norm.weight` / off 1323827200** (fourth reproduction), and detaching the source returned **win32 433 = ERROR_NO_SUCH_DEVICE**. Off-arm baseline: **4.5607 tok/s / stall 113.0 ms / hit 0.8957** (auto = 5,000 slots). All three DX §5.2 gates (startup pinned trial read / detach mirror on failure / dynamically detach after 3 consecutive runtime I/O errors) **were implemented and merged into main**; the default remains **off** | `p4_dual_source.md` §8; §3 60 |
| **The second read source finally has an A/B table (Track D5)** | **After moving the same J.ZAO SSD into a USB4 enclosure (`BusType NVMe`, removing the entire UAS bridge layer), all 12 cells ran without errors.** The final obstacle was **the startup probe**, not the drive: its 1 s window captured the first read from a just-awakened USB4 enclosure and measured a **3.77 GB/s drive as 0.03**. Weighted routing then gave it only **0.0%** of the bytes (mean latency for those 775 requests was **1315 ms**—low weight → sparse requests → drive stays asleep → every request pays for a wake-up, a self-reinforcing loop). Three independent measurements pin this down: `dx_probe` with 1 handle, QD 8, for 25 s gave **3.772 GB/s**; the same 1 s probe gave **3.74** on a **warm** drive and **0.03** on a **cold** drive. The fix **excludes warm-up bytes** (baseline taken after warm-up; default 1 s warm-up + 1 s measurement, `DEEPMOE_MIRROR_PROBE_WARMUP_MS`) ⇒ even a cold drive reports **3.30**, weights 4.82 : 3.30. Three ABAB pairs, two scripts, auto slots, **no weight override**: `y_turns` **4.7865 → 5.2368 = +9.40%** (clean off cell; nominal +12.50% because I compiled during one off cell and depressed the baseline, as recorded in §9.4), `long_turns` **5.8764 → 6.1488 = +4.63%** (both arms sd ≤0.11%). `nvme_stall` 113.9 → **95.4** / 72.7 → **65.1** ms; hit is bit-identical in both arms; aggregate reads 4.11 → **5.03 GB/s**. **Two streams** (new `bench/d5_ms_mirror_abab.py`, rotating the mirror rather than scheduling): **5.4507 → 6.1867 = +13.50%**, each stream 2.72 → 3.09; **MB/token did not change at all** (606/453 identical in both arms) ⇒ all the gain came from storage; aggregate disk rate 4.16 → **5.29 GB/s** | **GO.** All gates passed **with the mirror enabled**: `suite.decode` ✅, `suite.integration` ✅, `l3_ppl` off **NLL 0.630051 bit-identical**, device lost **0**, CPU `ctest` **46/46**. **The engine is still off by default** (`deepmoe serve` without `--mirror` is bit-identical); **the web UI enables it by default** (`find_mirrors()` in `server.py` automatically finds a copy at the same path on another drive with a manifest; `--no-mirror-auto` disables it). The 73:27 / 77:23 split **did not reach the bandwidth ratio of 56:44** because `src[1]`'s mean latency was **5–6 times** D:'s (149 vs 26 ms)—**routing accurately reflected a drive slower on bursts**. Forcing `DEEPMOE_MIRROR_WEIGHTS=4.72;3.66` landed at the same point (5.1046) ⇒ tuning bought nothing | `p4_dual_source.md` §9; §3 62 |
| **That 149 ms was never the drive (Track D6)** | §9.5 interpreted `src[1]` mean latency of 149 ms versus D:'s 26 ms as “E: is 5–6 times slower on bursts.” **The measurement mixed categories**: two classes were routed (P0 \| P3), and **E: carried 914 of 1,214 P3 backfills (75.3%)**. P0 preempts backfill, so backfill mean latency was **1,272 ms**—**D:'s backfill was also 1,138 ms**. That measures the engine queue, not the drive. **These backfills accounted for 97.3% of E:'s latency mass.** New per-source P0 counters: **E: P0 4.52 ms, D: P0 3.81 ms, a 1.19-fold difference**. D5's own `status.json` already showed **the slowest P0 in an entire cell was 19.84 ms** (17.66–39.89 across six on cells); **a 1-second wake-up could not be hidden there**. Thus keep-alive (a rotating 4 KiB read after 15 ms idle, **3,052 pokes / zero refused / zero errors**) gave **+0.15%**, with the split bit-identical at 28.5%; forcing an open-loop **44.0%** split with `DEEPMOE_MIRROR_STATIC_SPLIT=0.44` gave **−2.68%** (stall 95.1 → 100.9, `io: busy` 47.8 → 50.2 s, **P0 first-of-burst 1.15 → 1.27 ms**—spreading a layer's burst means **each layer must ramp up both drives**, while decode waits for the slower completion in that layer) | **Both NO-GO; the default is bit-identical to D5.** The 28.5% split **was not failing to reach 43%; 44% was actually worse**: steady-state bandwidth ratio is not decode's objective; **per-layer burst completion time is** | `p4_dual_source.md` §10; §3 63 |
| **Where the 0.40 ms gate round trip goes (Track G)** | Hot step with all experts resident, 40 layer steps, µs/layer: **GPU-side gap 386.1** (trace) = **220.9** after the host-side fence + **165.2** that cannot be measured directly (fence wake-up + submit → GPU start). Breakdown of the host's 220.9: **one `vkQueueSubmit2` call 99.5**, MoE staging 44.7, next-layer prologue (bind + record_attention, in the same gap) 32.6, `verify` + top-16 read **26.0**, MoE recording 9.7, planner 8.1, planner wait 0.35. **There is no top-k readback**—the gate kernel has already written host-coherent memory. A miss layer is entirely different: 8,780 µs after the fence, of which `pwait` is **8,493 = NVMe** | §3 53; `DEEPMOE_GATE_PROBE=1`, `tools/trace_timeline.py --gate` |
| Hot-step breakdown (64 token) | attention 36.0 / MoE GPU 29.4 / MoE host 1.0 / engram 3.9 / tail 5.8 / stall 0.4 / other 5.0 ms | `p2_decode.md` §10 |
| Hot step versus the design floor | Floor **75.8 ms**, measured 81.5 ms = **+7.5%**; only ~7% headroom remains | `design.md` §13.4; `p2_decode.md` §10 |
| **Submits per token** | **41** (P2 step 2 was ~128); one submit round trip **0.13–0.15 ms** | `p2_decode.md` §5.2/§10 |
| Resident bytes per token | **8.52 GB** (`hot_bytes`, agrees with design §2.3 to three digits); at 216 GB/s = **39.4 ms** | `p4_expert_patterns.md` §3 |
| Measured memory bandwidth ceiling | **215–218 GB/s** (path A 216.4 / path B 215.2 / DEVICE_LOCAL 216.0) = **84%** of LPDDR5X-8000's theoretical 256 | `kernel_p1.md` §2.2 |
| Effective hot-step bandwidth | **160 GB/s = 74% of 217** | `p4_summary.md` §2 |
| **Effective NVMe read bandwidth** | **8.3–9.2 GB/s**, the same at all four cache capacities | `p4_summary.md` §8 |
| Short-prompt TTFT | 5–23 s (11–46 tokens, using the decode path, 1.5–3 tok/s) | README |
| Long-prompt TTFT (GPU prefill, cold cache) | 64 / 4,133 / 17,010 tokens: **43 s / ≈150 s / ≈445 s**, compute ≈ **24 ms/prompt token** | `p3_prefill.md` §10.2 |
| **Long-prompt TTFT: GPU prefill is on by default (Track PF)** | `gpu_prefill_min` **0 → 512**. For a new 1,118-token prompt with 5,000 slots: **warm cache 168.8 s (151.0 ms/token, decode) → 28.6 s (25.6 ms/token, gpu) = 5.9×**; cold cache 176.7 → 39.2 s = 4.5×. Both original reasons in §6 for disabling it are gone: F4's path-A reservation allows allocation alongside 5,000 slots, and `PREFILL_HANDOFF` warms the cache as a side effect | `p3_chat.md` §8 |
| **Continuations also left the decode path (Track PF)** | The engine **still cannot prefill a suffix from a nonzero position** (blocker in §3 61), so it uses “**do the math, discard the reuse**”: reusing r of p tokens costs `p−r` decode tokens, while GPU prefilling the whole sequence again costs only `p/6`. Second turn of 1,514 tokens (reuse 1,116): **70.5 s → 35.5 s = 2.0×**. The output is bit-identical—this is what the turn would do with an empty store | `p3_chat.md` §8.3; `p4_kv_ux.md` §6 |
| **serve logs previously never reached `--log` (Track PF)** | `serve` reserves stdout for the protocol and calls `dup2(stderr, 1)`, but `core/log.h` writes to **`FILE* stdout`, fully buffered on a pipe**; `emit` flushes only at `>= Warn` ⇒ every `log_info` sat in an unflushed 4 KiB buffer and disappeared when the process was killed. The two lines in `web_serve.log` came directly from `fprintf(stderr,...)`. Fix: log sink + **flush each line**; serve calls `set_log_stream(stderr)`. The same file now has 37 startup lines | `p3_chat.md` §8.1 |
| **SSD KV prefix reuse** | Same 4,133-token prompt: cold **101.6 s** → SSD hit in a new process **2.47 s (41×)** | `p4_test_report.md` §5 |
| Correctness (against fp32 reference) | 64 token 8/8; 4K / 17K teacher-forced 8/8 and free-running 8/8 | `p4_test_report.md` §2 |
| tokenizer | **100% agreement** with HF `tokenizers` over 24,897 cases / 7.69 M IDs | README |
| Sampling | GPU top set + exact host check; **0 fallbacks** in 650 sampling steps | README |
| KV (in model format) | Measured **54.96 MiB** at position 17,010; 61 MB at 64K context and 0.94 GB at 1M context | `p4_summary.md` §6 |
| Bitwise deterministic decode | Same hash `a919aaf6…` across 3 processes | `p2_decode.md` §4.4 |
| **Cache default (`auto`)** | **5,100 slots / 89.3 GiB**: hit 0.9383, stall 76.0 ms/token, **5.603 tok/s** (eight-turn script, quiet machine) | `p4_hitrate.md` §4 (F4) |
| **`auto` now probes instead of only calculating (Track H1a, 2026-09-19)** | The computed budget gets another step: ① **hard cap of `kAutoSlotCap = 5,000` slots** (`DEEPMOE_CACHE_SLOT_CAP` adjusts it; 0 disables it), with both “computed” and “capped” values logged; ② **submit an empty command buffer after building the slab pool**—this is exactly when oversizing becomes visible (all allocations succeed; residency is checked only when work is queued). On refusal, **release the pool, reduce slots by 200 (`DEEPMOE_CACHE_BACKOFF_SLOTS`), and rebuild**, at most 5 times; ③ on device loss (`VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST`), **do not retry**, since a lost device cannot be reused in this process; report “next time use `--cache-slots N-200`.” `--cache-slots` / `--cache-gb` are probed too, but **are never silently reduced**; failure is fatal and reports the next number to try. Pure functions `cap_auto_budget` / `cache_backoff_slots` have CPU unit tests + 3 mutations (`suite.cache_cap`, `tests/mutate.py`); the new GPU-side `smoke.auto_cache` (`needs-model;needs-gpu`, start engine with auto + decode two tokens) **was not run this round** | `runtime/engine.cpp` `build_expert_cache_probed`; `tests/test_store.cpp` |
| Cache capacity ceiling (this machine) | **Safe ceiling: 5,000 slots** (**confirmed again on the afternoon of 2026-09-19, while `auto` today was above it**: `--cache-slots 5100` (34 A + 17 B) **failed on the first decode submit with `vkQueueSubmit2 failed (-2)`**; 5,000 (34 A + 16 B) completed four turns cleanly—§3 59). 5,400 passed three turns but failed partway through eight; **5,500 lost the device on the first token** (36 A + 19 B slabs, 53.8 GiB free). ~~5500 slots = 96.34 GiB usable~~ is invalidated | `p4_hitrate.md` §4 (F4) |
| Long-prompt TTFT (4,133 tokens, 4,500 slots) | **1,061.7 s** when GPU prefill was starved of path A; **100.0 s (10.6×)** after reserving 4 GiB of path A | `p4_hitrate.md` §5 (F4) |
| **Four resident-only routing levels (4 conversation turns, 5,100 slots)** | `off` 4.82 tok/s (mass lost 0, P0 262.5 GiB) / `stall1` 5.14 (0.048) / **`verify` 7.57 ×1.57 (0.1377, P0 62.5 GiB)** / `all` 8.99 ×1.87 (0.2587). Quality (64-step teacher-forced PPL, relative to `off`) is respectively 1.00 / 1.11 / **1.376** / 2.29—**all four levels are NO-GO as defaults** | `p4_resident_routing.md` §8.3 / §9.3 / **§10** |
| **Speculative decoding (DSpark), measured in the engine** | Serial decode **102.6 ms/position**; `forward_batch` M=6 **75.9 (0.74×)**, M=1 123.7 (1.21×). With the draft **free**, per emitted token: `chain` k=5 **0.86× (slower)**, k=1 1.06–1.13×; `longest` k=5 **≤1.07×**, and `longest` **is not lossless**. Adding `T_draft` at 19–42 ms/cycle leaves all cases tied or worse—**NO-GO on this machine** | `p4_dspark_runtime.md` §7; §3 41 / 42 |
| **Where one verify forward pass spends its time (Track BF, 2026-09-21)** | The batch path **previously had no per-dispatch timestamps**; now it does. M=5 verify, all experts resident (P0 = 0), 970 dispatches/batch: **MoE union 238.5 ms = 73%** (busy 168.9 + gap 69.6), attention chain 55.7 = 17%, engram 22.3 = 7%, tail 9.0, ced 2.2. Per-slot cost: M=1 **0.115 ms** versus M=5 union **0.207 ms**—**the union does not reread weights per column, but each slot costs 1.8 times as much**; the attention chain is nearly flat with M (weight-outer / batch-inner holds) | `p4_dspark_runtime.md` §8.2 |
| **Reading x back in `forward_batch` (Track BF)** | In the MoE gap, **33.3 ms/batch was spent reading 4 MB from write-combined memory with ordinary loads = 124 MB/s**. Switching to **`vmovntdqa`** (`_mm256_stream_load_si256`, `wc_read()`): **33.3 → 5.2 ms/batch**; the best cell on the M verify curve moved from **69.8 (M=5) → 65.2 ms/position (M=6)**, and the curve **no longer rises again** after M ≥ 4. At M=1, `forward_batch` **ties serial decode** (96.7 vs 96.3; the “1.21×” claim in §3 42 is invalidated). Numerical results are bit-identical (cos 0.9398023 / top-1 54/60 / PPL 0.9858×). **No measurable change to M=1 decode steps** (only 20 KB per layer, small enough for the shadow copy) | `p4_dspark_runtime.md` §8.3, §8.6 |
| **M1 gate (`forward_batch` vs M=1)** | **Not bit-identical**: cos = 1.000000000 for the first three layers; the first gate flip occurs at L07. Across 60 positions, worst cos **0.9398**, top-1 **54/60**. Quality is unchanged (teacher-forced PPL 1.8589 vs 1.8857 = 0.986×). Thus `suite.spec_forward` **passes with WARN**, failing only when cos < 0.93 or top-1 < 50/60 | §3 41 |
| **Where P0 disk time goes (Track Q1)** | 4 conversation turns, 5,100 slots, backfill off: `nvme_stall` **105.2 ms/token**, 21.3 misses = **383 MiB/token** ⇒ **3.55 GB/s**, while `nvme_bench` on the same drive reports **5.16 GB/s**. **At issue, 0.0% of P0 requests had non-P0 chunks in flight** (only 8.2% and a mean of 1.02 even with backfill on)—**queue contention = 0**. The first P0 in a layer's batch takes **2.83 ms**, each later one **8.46 ms**; mean in-flight chunks at issue: **3.80** (limit 8) | `p4_p0_queue.md` §2 |
| **Where that 30% gap is (Track Q2)** | **In `ReadFile` itself, not the drive.** A synchronous 4 MiB `ReadFile` into path A (`DEVICE_LOCAL\|HOST_VISIBLE`, uncached) takes **704 µs**, versus **70 µs** into ordinary host memory (69.6 µs into imported path B)—the kernel must probe and then lock the destination's 1,024 pages. This used to happen **only on the single dispatcher thread**: of 42.81 s spent issuing, **42.71 s was in `Backend::submit`**; completion callbacks took only 0.13 s (1.6 µs each). **Moving submission to 8 threads + P0 queue 24 / 96 MiB: 4.828 → 4.998 tok/s (+3.5%)**, stall 108.1 → 100.9 ms, busy-window rate 3.90 → 4.10 GB/s. **Q1's reference point was also wrong**: by default, `nvme_bench` measures `%TEMP%` (C:), while the shard is on **D:**; D:'s ceiling is **4.60–4.65 GB/s, not 5.16**, and its rate **is not flat across request sizes** | `p4_p0_queue.md` §9–§13 |

| **System throughput with two concurrent conversations (Track MS)** | Two decode streams in one engine process, interleaved by layer (`pipeline` mode of `Engine::decode_step_multi`): combined **4.6474 → 5.4602 tok/s (+17.5%)**, three ABAB pairs, inter-cell sd ≤0.83%; per-stream rate 4.415/4.906 → 2.730 tok/s (**0.62×, throughput traded for latency**). **Token-level ping-pong (D2) was −5.1%**, so the gain really comes from overlap. Aggregate disk rate **2.21 → 2.83 GB/s (+28%)**, but two working sets competing for one 5,100-slot LRU reduced `y_turns` hit by **1.6 pt** and raised MB/token by **13.5%**, consuming half the gain; **N≥3 is negative** (5,100 slots cannot hold three working sets) | `p4_multistream.md` §5 |

| **Eviction policy as a lever (Track E1)** | **Dropped.** Swept 127 configurations under a 4.6 GB/s demand-only model: the best implementable policy was `last_use + α × heat` (α ≈ 1,600–4,800), **+2.3%** on the C=5,100 test set (hit 0.9113 → 0.9151), **+1.2%** after halving, below the ±3% noise band. **The top-16 “near-hit” score contributed only 0.06 pt (16%) of that**. Running `score-aware` from `cache_sim` unchanged gave **−68%** (sorting by heat degenerates). Belady was **+40%** by the same measure—that 40% lives in “the future,” not in the score | `p4_cache_policy.md` §12; §3 47 |

**One-sentence conclusion**: `tok/s ≈ NVMe_eff / (MB per token)`. Effective read bandwidth stays at 8.3–9.2 GB/s across four capacities;
hit 0.59 → 0.84 cuts MB/token from 4,998 to 2,269, doubling tok/s.
**Performance in this range is determined entirely by “how many bytes must be read per token”**, not by the kernel (§4).

---

## 2. The optimisation path: every step and what attributed it

### 2.1 The decode hot step: 134 ms → 81.5 ms (Track I, `p2_decode.md` §10)

| Step | per token, before → after | The measurement that attributed it |
|---|---|---|
| Baseline (P2 step 2) | **134.0 ms**, ~128 submits | Breakdown: attention 51.9 / MoE ~68 / tail 8.4 |
| **Cut the command buffer only at the gate** (one layer's MoE and the next layer's attention in the same submit) | ~128 → **41 submits** | `Engine::measure_submit_overhead` = **0.13–0.15 ms a round trip** × ~128 = **~17 ms/token = 13% of the hot step**. The residency gate becomes a semaphore wait inside the submit, and the host fence moves to a second timeline |
| **One MoE runner, seven slots** (6 FP4 routed + the fp8 shared one at spare index 384, `HQuant=3`) | MoE 1.72 → 0.73 ms/layer | §5.2 measured MoE at 1.72 ms/layer while `kernel_p2_moe`'s kernel needs only 0.68 -- **~0.75 ms of that is the host's fp32 addition and two 20 KB write-combining reads**. "The cheapest 40 ms" |
| Vectorised host `act_quant` (branch-free `fp8_round` + F16C) | MoE host ~28 → 6.4 → **1.0 ms** | A bit-for-bit self-check of `cpu::act_quant_block` over 5,120 values |
| Fixed three `VkCommandBuffer`s that were allocated on every call and never freed | — | 40 leaked per token (`run_attention` / `run_close` / `EngramRunner::run`) |
| **Fetch the engram rows at the start of the token**, overlapped with layer 0's attention; cache the RoPE tables | engram 4.9 → **3.9 ms** | One submit fewer per engram layer; all 40 `bind` calls were computing a RoPE table each, while a step needs only three |
| **Move the FFN input to cached host pages (path B)** | −6.2 ms/token | That one 20 KB activation the host reads every layer used to live in path A's write-combining memory, and **reading it measured 155 µs a layer** |
| **Total** | **134.0 → 86.7 → 81.5 ms = 12.3 tok/s** | Repeats: 81.5 / 81.9 / 81.8 / 83.2 / 81.7 / 83.2 ms |
| **decode takes the M=1 specialised MoE pipeline** (Track K1a, 2026-09-19, on today's `auto` baseline) | hot step **101.8 → 97.0 ms**, moe gpu **37.87 → 34.33** | The engine's runner is `spec.m = 6` (for the verify batch) and decode only sets `live_columns = 1` -- six accumulators computing one column. Three ABAB pairs, A = `DEEPMOE_MOE_STATIC_M1=0`; `plan_p5.md` §3(h) |

Against the floors: non-MoE, a floor of 42.2 against 41.8 measured (**already on the floor**); MoE GPU 25.0–27.3 against 29.4
(the 7th slot is a real 23.6 MB fp8 expert, not a 12.5 MB FP4 stand-in); engram 4.9 against 3.9.
**What remains is structural**: 40 host round trips per token to read the gate (~1.6 ms of submit + ~2 ms of fence latency),
and MoE's `x` making a trip out to the host and back every layer.

### 2.2 attention: 1,071 → 696 µs/layer

| Stage | One layer (dispatches 1–9) | 40 layers + head | What was done |
|---|---|---|---|
| P2 step 1 | **1,071 µs / 124 GB/s** | 48.5 ms | baseline |
| P2 step 2 | **892.6 µs / 149 GB/s** | 41.4 ms | A per-shader LDS budget; the act_quant staging spread over the whole workgroup; a per-stage `WaveReduce`; `sparse_attn` vectorised (141 → 69 µs) |
| + the LDS bank-conflict fix | **797–804 µs** | 37.6–37.8 ms | **One word of padding** (`kXStride = 17`) -- a pure layout change, output bit for bit identical |
| + K-split, 8-tile attention | 709–711 µs | 34.0 ms | |
| **+ K-split, 32×1 tiles (the P3 default)** | **696.3–696.8 µs / 191 GB/s = 88% of 217** | **33.5 ms** | The K-split defaults: wq_a 2 / wkv 2 / wo_a 4 / wo_b 8, **one row per lane** |

`wo_b` step by step: 312–314 µs (62%) → +a 17-word LDS stride **259–266 (73–75%)**
→ +arithmetic decode / 4 accumulators / folded scale **258–272 (no change)** → **+K-split ×8, one row per lane: 203.5–205.4 µs / 204–206 GB/s (94–95%)**;
K-split ×16 goes back up to 216–220.

Two bugs each worth 50× were fixed along the way: **a local array sized by a push constant lands in VRAM**
(Sinkhorn **216 µs → 2.8 µs**, the gate rank loop 55 → 2 µs); and **a single-lane serial argmax** (the gate's top-k **56 µs → 12.6 µs**).

⚠️ **Track J's path has not been adopted by the runtime** (`design.md` §15.2).
And the LDS bank-conflict fix **cannot be seen on the real machine**: attention was 36.0 ms in Track I and 36.9 ms after Track Q,
while J claimed −3.8 ms and "already in effect". That is an unresolved contradiction (limit 6.5).

### 2.3 The MoE kernel (`kernel_p1.md` / `kernel_p2_moe.md`)

| M | Best variant | A+B GB/s | of the ceiling | ms/pair | **ms/token** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `L32 R1 xglob` | 222.6 | **102%** | 0.591 | **0.5912** |
| 2 | `L32 R1 xgf16` | 206.4 | 95% | 0.637 | 0.3187 |
| 4 | `L16 R2 xgf16` | 162.0 | 74% | 0.813 | 0.2031 |
| 6 | `L16 R2 xgf16/ldsi8` | 135.3 | 62% | 0.972 | **0.1621** |

ms/token **0.5912 → 0.1621 = 3.65×** -- **this is what speculative decoding actually buys** (more tokens for the same weights).

The three things kept: **packed fp16 (`XMode=4`) on dispatch A only** (M=6 A 117.2 → 133.9 GB/s, +14%; error 4.52e-4, well inside 1e-3, "adoptable unconditionally");
**int8 dot4 (`xldsi8`) on dispatch B only** (M=6 B 100.0 → 125.7, +26%);
and **`HQuant=3`** (h quantisation moved into a third small dispatch) -- which takes decode's quantisation tax from **6.3 ms/token down to ≈1.0 ms/token**,
bit for bit identical to `HQuant=2` (5.030e-08); M=1's +4.2% is entirely that extra dispatch and its global barrier.

**The diagnostic turning point**: P1's "M=6 is held up by activation load instructions" was **wrong**. `t_A(M) ≈ 0.33 + 0.055·M` ms
matches an instruction count (165 M FMA + 82.6 M `f16tof32`, 7.4 Top/s, 61% VALU occupancy):
**M≥3 is VALU-issue bound, not memory bound**. Every memory-saving design failed and every instruction-saving one worked (§3 1/2/9).
The exit criterion "≥80% of raw-read" is itself wrong for a VALU-bound kernel, and was replaced with `ms/token ≤ 0.17` (0.158 measured).

### 2.4 Hit rate and capacity -- decode's real lever

| Configuration | Slots | cache | decode tok/s | hit | MB/token | effective NVMe |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| cache-1000 | 1000 | 17.5 GiB | 1.830 | 0.5912 | 4,998 | 9.14 GB/s |
| cache-2200 | 2200 | 38.5 GiB | 2.593 | 0.7431 | 3,528 | 9.15 GB/s |
| cache-4500 | 4500 | 78.8 GiB | 3.647 | 0.8370 | 2,269 | 8.28 GB/s |
| **8 turns, 5500 slots** | 5500 | **96.34 GiB** | **6.05** | **0.9431** | — | stall 46–71 ms |

**Every doubling of capacity is worth +0.093 of hit** (0.5912 → 0.7431 → 0.8370).
The 0.9431 measured at 5500 slots agrees with `cache_sim`'s 0.9451 at 5711 -- **the gain comes from capacity, not from policy**.

**F4 (2026-09-18, `p4_hitrate.md` §0/§3/§4) closed the "policy" side for good: the engine is plain LRU.**
Replaying a run's own `route.bin` through `cache_sim`'s LRU, the engine and the simulator agree at **every step**
(4,500 slots 2,489/2,489; 5,000 slots 2,489/2,489; the 4-turn `config_sweep` 214/214).
The premise that "the engine is 6.8 points below plain LRU" **does not exist** -- that compared a 4-turn, 92-step cold-cache run
against an 8-turn, 2,193-step one. An ablation with `BACKFILL` / `PREFILL_HANDOFF` / `MOE_OVERLAP` all turned off
confirms it from the other side: hit 0.9250 → 0.9252, MB/token 338.3 → 337.8, **there is nothing to turn off**.
The capacity curve is **still climbing** at this machine's ceiling (sim: 5,100 → 0.9383, 6,500 → 0.9532), so **every point of hit is throughput**.

Routing facts (`p4_expert_patterns.md`): `prefill_hit = 0.7607`, and **86.6% of the experts decode picks have already appeared in the prompt's prefill**
→ the prefill→decode handover is the largest single lever. Hit rate drops because **the topic changes, not because the context gets longer**
(within one topic, hit is 0.94–0.955 per 128 steps; across topics 0.877–0.90; stall 58 ms against 151 ms).
**There is no small hot core**: the experts appearing in ≥50% of decode steps are 0.025 per layer = 0.2% of events; ~380 of 384 experts get touched.
Temporal reuse is at the 128-step scale (63.7%), and only 15.4% between adjacent tokens -- **prefetching for the adjacent token is pointless**.

### 2.5 prefill

| N | TTFT | expert NVMe wait | compute | the design §7.13.3 model |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 64 | **43.2 s** | 33.7 s (94 GB) | 8.2 s | ≈34 s |
| 4,133 | **199.6 s (≈150 s on a quiet machine)** | 44.5 s (198 GB) | 100 s | ≈62–65 s |
| 17,010 | **628.6 s (≈445 s on a quiet machine)** | 28.2 s (203 GB) | 396 s | ≈78–88 s |

**What the model missed is compute: 24 ms/prompt token, linear, where the prior allowed only 0.7 ms**;
§7.13.4's criterion 4 (±15%) fails until it is refitted as `T_compute(N) ≈ 8 s + 0.024·N`.
A 17K prefill is **compute-bound, not NVMe-bound**.

**prefill's one large remaining item**: the host's top-6 reads the gate bias through device-mapped memory ~3,000 times a token --
**58 s of a 4K prefill (1.45 s a layer) → 1.1 s**, just by copying 384 floats once a layer.

**The bigger thing F4 found: GPU prefill could not allocate a single byte (`p4_hitrate.md` §5).**
The slab pool fills path A until `vkAllocateMemory` refuses, so **past a cache of ≥ 3,600 slots,
GPU prefill -- which allocates later and only accepts path A -- cannot even get 21 MB**, and silently falls back to the decode path.
That is why every cell in this document used to report `prefill_mode: "decode"`.
The fix is for `Engine::build_expert_cache` to hold a **4 GiB path A reservation** while it builds the pool (`kPathAReserve`,
in 2 GiB pieces, because 4 GiB at once hits `maxMemoryAllocationSize`), released as soon as the pool is built.
It costs two slabs, and it buys: **a 4,133-token prompt's prefill 1,061.7 s → 100.0 s (10.6×),
prefill throughput 3.89 → 41.3 tok/s, the whole cell 19 min → 3 min**.

Track S's coopmat: at N=4133, **legacy 103.67 s against coop 99.89 s, only 3.6% faster**, and the 5× target was not met.

**F3 (`p4_prefill_speed.md` v1.0) explained why that 3.6% was so small, and why 5× is out of reach.**
That coopmat was **correct** (all 110 stage checks pass), but its **P·V stretch had a bad access order** --
it indexed the output by dim and reduced over entries, so one dim tile walked the aggregated KV plane in 16 KiB strides
and **re-read it 32 times**: 13,119 ms → **3,032 ms**. Three others were likewise **grid or loop order** rather than arithmetic:
each coopmat GEMM was fixed at 2 token tiles per workgroup (at n=512 that walks `wq_b`'s 84 MB 16 times),
the mHC pre-norm used **one thread per row** (4,133 threads, each walking 20,480 floats),
and the gate's top-6 read n × 384 scores back to the host through a device-mapped mapping and then `partial_sort`ed them;
plus `wo_a` -- the only large linear still on a tiled GEMV, because `op_gemm` refuses the coopmat branch for a **grouped** linear.

After those fixes, 4,133 tokens' **compute is 80.6 s = 19.5 ms/prompt token**
(band attention 38.2 / routed expert GPU 27.1 / mHC 4.9 / shared expert 3.2 / engram 2.8 / gate+route 2.5 / everything else 1.9).

**≥ 5× is below this machine's arithmetic floor, and that is a sentence you can stop on**: at replay 128 this prompt is
**73.5 TFLOP** (36.2 of it routed expert alone), while the best rate **any** kernel reached during that prefill is
**2.1 TFLOP/s** (the shared expert, the cleanest large GEMM; routed experts only manage 1.15–1.34).
5 ms/prompt token = 20.7 s = **3.56 TFLOP/s sustained**, 1.7 times the best rate.
So **the target is not reachable by computing less** -- the seven de-work/overlap items in §6 add up to 12–15 s.
The only thing that can move the rate is rewriting `prefill_coopmat` stage 0 into
**several waves cooperating on one output tile through LDS, with the next K slice prefetched** (today it is one 32-lane wave per workgroup,
tiles read straight out of global memory, with no LDS staging and no double buffering). **That was F3's recommendation, and the thing it did not do.**

---

## 3. Tried and reverted (numbered, 92 entries)

This section is the most useful part of this document. **Estimates run systematically high** (over on fleet it is the "rule of one half";
for the same phenomenon here see 23, 25, 30), so **halve** any byte-count-based estimate before deciding whether to spend a day on it.

### 3.1 MoE kernel

| # | Experiment | Measured | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **LDS x-tiling** (`XMode=1/2`, move one K block of the activation into LDS and share it across all M columns) | M=6 L16R2: `xglob` **119.0 GB/s / 0.1844 ms/token** → `xlds` **95.5 / 0.2297**; dispatch B on its own **100.0 → 72.5 (−27%)**; `xldsf16` is worse still at 76.3. L32R1: 110.4 → **42.6** | **Reverted, rule deleted**. Cause: two `GroupMemoryBarrierWithGroupSync()` per K-chunk drain the memory pipeline (A has 10 blocks per wg, B 35); `ds_read_b128` and `global_load_dwordx4` both read 16 B, so the instruction count did not go down. design §7.1 rule 6 is rewritten to "use LDS only when it saves instructions, never with a per-K-chunk barrier". **The one exception**: `xldsi8` on dispatch B only (125.7 vs 100.0), kept |
| **2** | **int8 pre-quantised x (`XMode=6`)** -- §3.6 once asserted this was "the only design that can reach 85%" | dispatch A steadily **+15…+30%** (M=6 A 129.6 → **168.1 GB/s**); but end-to-end ms/token: M=1 **0.597 → 0.692 (+15.9%)**, M=2 −6.5%, M=6 over three rounds −7.0% / +0.7% / −0.6%. A+B at M=6 is still only 137–147 GB/s = 63–67% of the ceiling (the bottleneck moved to dispatch B, whose activation is `h`, not `x`). Accuracy: expert (39,383) **8.85e-3**, over design §12's ≤5e-3 | **Implemented, spec constant defaults to 0 (off)**; "never let this become the decode default". The assertion in §7.9.2(d) is retracted |
| **3** | **"splitting the dispatch costs +0.193 ms per layer"** | v0.1: 7 slots at once vs 3+4, M=1 **0.596 → 0.789 (+32%)** ⇒ "7.7 ms per token, §7.9's 'negligible' was wrong by 340×". v0.2 re-measured with a **rotating configuration**: the real cost is **+0.021…+0.047 ms/layer**, and "split A only" is **+0.006…+0.026** (one row is still −0.041). 40 layers = **0.2–1.0 ms/token, not 7.7** | **The v0.1 conclusion is retracted**. Self-incriminating evidence: the old section measured `whole 0.652 < A 0.520 + B 0.290`, which is physically impossible -- the three numbers come from three different thermal states (8% drift within a phase, 7% run-to-run). **"First-arrived, first-computed" is restored as the default, only dispatch A is split, and B runs last** (bit for bit identical: 5120/5120, 30720/30720 words) |
| **4** | **`HQuant=2` (`hq8`, dispatch A writes fp8 h directly)** | Numerically correct (5.030e-08), but it forces a 32-row workgroup, which pushes M=1 from `L32 R1` down to `L16 R2`: **0.591 → 0.749 ms/pair (+27%) = 6.3 ms/token**. `hqB` (`HQuant=1`) is worse: M=1 **+45.5%**, M=6 `L32R1 xgf16 hqB` **+248%** | Both are superseded by `HQuant=3` (§2.3). "There is now no reason at all to use `HQuant=2`". The companion LDS-transpose scheme was **proved impossible** (an `L32 R1` workgroup owns only 8 rows, while one fp8 block needs 32 rows) |
| **5** | fp32 `h` | Output error 1.37e-4 → 1.15e-4 (16% better), but **9% slower** (198.5 GB/s vs 218.5) | Reverted, h stays fp16 |
| **6** | **arithmetic / select-tree FP4 decode** (`dec1` / `dec2`) | M=1 A+B: constant table **218.5 GB/s / 0.602 ms**, arithmetic **164.2 / 0.802**, select tree **151.4 / 0.869** -- a spread of 1.4× | Counter-intuitive, "only measurement can speak". **Table decode is the one knob you have to get right** |
| **7** | `RowsPerLane ≥ 4` (M=1 / dispatch A) | M=1: R1 218.5 > R2 197.2 > R4 189.8. M=6 dispatch A collapses at R≥4: L16 R2 130.6 → R4 87.6 → R8 38.4 → **R16 11.0 GB/s** (register spill) | The earlier conclusion that "R helps dispatch B" is **overturned** by a clean re-measurement. A and B share R; giving each its own spec constant is estimated at <2% and has **never been done** (design §15 issue 6) |
| **8** | `LanesPerRow` 16/32/64 and Wave32/Wave64 | LanesPerRow differs by only 4% (32 is best); Wave32 vs Wave64 shows **no systematic difference (1–5%, mixed directions)** | design §7.1 rule 3 ("16 lanes/row, 256 B granularity is better") is **unconfirmed**; rule 4 ("prefer Wave32") is **neither supported nor refuted** |
| **9** | 2 MiB large pages for path B | `VirtualAlloc(MEM_LARGE_PAGES)` needs `SeLockMemoryPrivilege`, which the account does not have; the code falls back to 4 KiB and records the reason | **Not measured / blocked by the environment**. The potential prize is path B's 12% MoE-kernel deficit |

### 3.2 attention kernel

| # | Experiment | Measured | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **10** | **`heads_per_wg` ("read KV only once")** | heads/wg = 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 → score+combine **69 / 100 / 169 / 288 µs** (L2 traffic 41 / 20 / 10 / 5 MB). Re-measured after the LDS fix: **54 / 76 / 117 / 209 µs** | **Never adopt**. 8 heads/wg = 8 workgroups spread over 40 CUs -- trading traffic along the wrong axis. Use a head-group × KV-tile grid instead |
| **11** | `pv_heads_per_wg = 8` in tiled attention | **120 µs** vs 25–27 µs for 1 | Rejected. P·V cannot share a single KV read (one lane owns the 16 **output** dims of **one** head) |
| **12** | arithmetic fp8 E4M3 decode in the attention GEMV (`DEEPMOE_FP8_ARITH`) | Exact (all 256 codes identical, L2 output bit for bit identical) but slower: `wq_a` 38.1 → **57.5 µs (+51%)**, `wq_b` +14%, `wkv.gemv` +35%; one layer 799.5 → 861.8 µs | Off. **A randomly indexed 256-entry LDS table still beats six ALU instructions** |
| **13** | four accumulators per block (`DEEPMOE_GEMV_ACC=4`) | `wo_b` 259.0 / 266.5 µs (one) vs 271.8 / 259.2 (four): **no difference** | Off |
| **14** | folding the UE8M0 weight scale into the staged block factor (`DEEPMOE_GEMV_FOLD_SCALE=1`) | `wo_b` 267.7 / 271.9: **no difference**; combined with K-split it is **worse** (`wo_b.ksplit×2`: **411 µs vs 303**) | Off |
| **15** | **"the `act_quant` round trip is `wo_b`'s bottleneck" -- falsified** | Compiled with `ActQuant=0` (the arithmetic is wrong, only the time is looked at): **150.2 GB/s vs 151.7 with it on** | The pre-quantised dispatch that was supposed to be the structural fix **buys nothing** |
| **16** | K-split with two rows per lane | The first measurement **lost three of the four kernels** (`wo_b` **304 µs vs 261**); `wo_a` ×1 (split kernel but not split) **218.6 µs / 154 GB/s vs 194.2 / 173** | It only wins with **one row per lane** (§2.2 keeps that) |
| **17** | a tighter LDS budget ("as small as K") | `wq_a` **152 GB/s at a 5120 budget vs 166 at 8192**; `wo_a` goes the same way | More resident workgroups each re-staging their own copy of a wide activation = more L2 traffic. Only `wq_b` / `indexer.wq_b` (K=1280) benefit |
| **18** | changing `row_reduce` to a single `WaveActiveSum` | `wq_b` **+30%**, `wkv` **+20%**, but `wq_a` and `wo_a` **−14%** | Made into a per-stage specialisation constant (`WaveReduce`); **no rule predicts who wins** (design §15 issue 11). On the four K-split stages there is "no measurable change" |

| **20** | **MoE live-column mask (`pc.m` / `set_live_columns`)** | The microbench is real: M=1 MoE **1.786 → 1.044 ms/layer (1.7×, 40 layers ≈30 ms/token)**. End-to-end A/B (4 rounds, 4500 slots): tok/s **3.647 → 3.536**, hit 0.8370 = 0.8370, MB/token 2,269.3 = 2,269.3, TTFT identical round by round | **No measurable difference (±3% jitter)**. Cause: a 30 ms/token kernel up against a **274 ms** token (2,269 MB ÷ 8.3 GB/s ≈ 273 ms I/O floor) -- the kernel is only **~2%**. Kept in the kernel, but **every "compute faster" change ranks behind "read less"** |
| **21** | **per-turn reheat** (decay heat at turn boundaries + rerun the P3 backfill) | 2200 slots, three turns on the same topic, on vs off: turn 3 **decode hit 0.7210 = 0.7210**. 4500 slots: hit **+0.0016 (noise)**, tok/s **−0.018**, **MB/token +32** (the 68 × 18.8 MB = 1.28 GB per turn it fetched itself) | **No gain, and slightly negative**. On a **saturated cache it is a no-op by construction** (`free_slots = 0` at every reheat). Do not turn it on for short conversations |
| **21b** | (a by-product of 21, two real bugs) | (a) **expert heat on the decode path is identically 0** -- `RouteDecision.near_ids/near_scores` was never assigned, so the EWMA loop of design §9.3 never ran, and "any score-aware strategy up to now was sorting all 0s". Fixed. (b) `decay_heat(0.5)` presses the whole heat field to 0 after a few rounds, so the absolute 5% floor then judges **every** expert cold. Fixed (the hot end is renormalised back to 1.0) | Recorded here because: **the real output of a "no gain" experiment is the two bugs it exposed** |
| **22** | multi-round auto-tune / `DEEPMOE_HEAT_FILE` sequential learning | round 0 hit **0.8278** / 3.33 tok/s → round 1 (reading round 0's heat) **0.8275** / 3.32 | The loop works, but on a short cold load of 3 turns / 190 tokens there is **no improvement** (every round starts from a cold cache). Needs longer idle backfill / longer conversations / cache retention across turns |
| **23** | **Lookahead routing prefetch (design §9.4)** | On a 27,399-token trace **every single (d,K) is net negative**. Baseline (4,787 slots, global LRU, no prefetch) **hit 0.8968 / 165.7 ms/token / 6.03 tok/s**. The best setting (`head`, d=4,K=8): effective hit 0.9052 (+0.8 points) but **282.3 ms/token / 3.54 tok/s**, NVMe bytes **+60%**. The worst (`tail`, d=6,K=16): **1036.3 ms/token / 0.96 tok/s, precision 0.000** | **Moved to design §16 as explicitly not-to-do**, `PrefetchConfig::lookahead_depth = 0`. "A probe inserted at the LRU tail is self-defeating", now nailed down as the test `test_probe_at_tail_is_self_defeating`; **do not copy it into `store/planner.cpp`**. The predictor's recall@6 is 0.66 at d=1 and 0.52 at d=3, with no knee out to d=8; and the zero-cost baseline "what the previous token picked" (Jaccard 0.239) already equals d=8 lookahead |
| **24** | statically pinning routed experts / per-layer quotas / score-aware / LFU-decay / ARC | LRU = LFU-decay = ARC are indistinguishable to four decimal places; score-aware wins only **0.20–0.23 points**; global beats per-layer by **0.6–0.8 points**; `static-pin + LRU` is **0.4–2.7 points worse at every capacity** | Global LRU, no per-layer quotas, static pin deleted, score-aware made a switch that defaults to off |
| **25** | **CPU sharing the expert GEMV (old P6)** | The memory controller is a single shared ~217 GB/s ceiling: GPU alone 216, CPU alone 100.9, concurrent **GPU 185.8 + CPU 28.6 = 214.4** -- **67–68%** of the sum of the two alone | Moved to design §16 as explicitly not-to-do |
| **26** | **"quantise/transform at expert load time"** (2026-09-17 audit) | Audited `Planner::fetch` → `iocp.cpp` → `ExpertStore::finish_run`: one `ReadFile` (overlapped, `FILE_FLAG_NO_BUFFERING`) straight into the slot's host pointer, and the completion callback only drives the state machine -- **there is no per-element loop anywhere on that path** | **There was never any quantisation on this path, so there is nothing to save**. The real suspect is **20–31 ms per miss** (107 GB / 5,030 fills) against ~2 ms of real disk time at 8.3–9.2 GB/s -- the next step is to instrument both sides of `ReadFile`, not to optimise a loop that does not exist |
| **27** | pre-recorded per-token command buffer ("dispatch overhead panic") | design §3.4 guessed 5–20 µs per dispatch + barrier; measured **0.56–0.66 µs** (GPU), and **1.1 µs** for the host to record one A+B pair. ~470 dispatches × 0.66 µs = **0.31 ms/token = 0.5% of 65 ms**, not 5–14% | A pre-recorded buffer is **no longer a performance necessity** (still wanted as a way to express the timeline) |
| **28** | a bigger cache within 8 steps | `--cache-gb 48` (2,700 slots, more than the 1,920 pairs these 8 steps even touch) gives hit **0.349**, no better than 12 GiB's 0.360 | Eight steps can never reach steady state. **The minimum length for any cache experiment is 128 steps** (§2.4's ramp) |

### 3.4 memory path / capacity

| # | Experiment | Measured | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **29** | **BIOS VGM (variable graphics memory) tuning** | `kernel_p1.md` §4.1 originally recommended "minimum VGM + path B" (path B cache ~90–95 GB vs path A's ~56 GB at VGM=64, at a cost of 40 layers × 0.081 ms = **3.2 ms/token**). Reason for the retraction: **capacity = VGM + (visible physical memory − headroom), and the total is conserved** -- changing VGM only changes the A/B split, not the total. And the two heaps are the same LPDDR5X anyway (path A 216.4 / path B 215.2 / DEVICE_LOCAL 216.0 GB/s, raw read differing by <0.5%) | **Plan retracted, the reboot was never needed**. "Any entry that says 'retest under minimum VGM' remains void" (design §16). **Do not raise this one again** |
| **30** | path B for the MoE kernel | The 12% that raw-read cannot see: A **221.8–222.1 GB/s / 0.592–0.593 ms**, B **193.6–198.2 / 0.664–0.680**, fully reproducible over six alternating rounds, while raw-read is 214.8–217.6 on both paths. Cause: the GART 4 KiB page walk under MoE's 7-expert × 2304-row access pattern. In the other direction: path B's NVMe landing is **+22%** (4.8 vs 3.9 GB/s) | **The kernel uses path A, path B is only for capacity overflow**. Sizing path B purely by physical memory blows up: with 50 GB free it wants 38 GiB, the slab import returns `VK_ERROR_INVALID_EXTERNAL_HANDLE` at ~33 GiB, and **the next submit finds the device lost**; `VK_EXT_memory_budget` reports host heap 35.4 GiB / **0 B in use** no matter what is running. **path B is capped at 16 GiB** |
| **31** | non-temporal stores into path A | 20.1 → 21.5 GB/s, **within noise** (the path A mapping is write-combining anyway). Only path B benefits (18.3 → **32.7 GB/s, 1.8×**) | Both are far above NVMe's 4.7 GB/s, so it is **irrelevant** |

### 3.5 speculative decoding (DSpark)

| # | Experiment | Measured | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **32** | **tree sampling (K=16 lattice) as a speedup** | Greedy, runtime replay tokens/verify: single chain k=1..5 **1.83 / 2.59 / 2.89 / 3.33 / 3.33**; tree K=16 `eal` **1.83 / 2.50 / 2.85 / 3.33 / 3.33**. E[tokens] per event at k=5: single chain **3.93**, K=4 3.93, K=8 3.87, **K=16 3.82**, K=32 3.87. Sampling (temperature 1): single chain 1.78/2.38/2.74/3.05/3.22, tree 1.77/2.39/2.70/3.02/3.22. TPS: **greedy −0…−4%, sampling ±1%** | **NO-GO as a speedup**. Kept only as the lossless CPU-side comparator at temperature 1 (`accept_sampling_exact`) and for the confidence computation; the path target falls back to `chain` (= single chain). Rejected along with it: the `base` candidate (acceptance **1.65 vs 2.93**, the trial was stopped after 6 minutes); `union2`/`union4` (coverage rises to 3.99/4.13 but the selected path **is not any better**: 2.87/2.85 vs 2.93); exact full-vocabulary normalisation (it changes 1–13% of the events' paths but the acceptance length by **≤0.1 token**, "it would need 1+4K GEMVs of 66 MB, not worth it") |
| **33** | **MoE column-wise dispatch (scheme A) as DSpark's landing path** | Per-layer MoE (layer 0, 6 disjoint experts per column, all resident): M=1 **1.786 ms/layer**, M=2 3.615 (2.02×), M=4 7.484 (4.19×), M=6 **11.674 (6.54×)** -- **zero amortisation**. One verify layer ≈ C(M) 4.7 + MoE 11.7 ≈ **16 ms**, 40 layers ≈ **640 ms**; running the same 6 tokens sequentially at M=1 is 40 × (1.3+1.8) = **124 ms**. **Column-wise verify is about 5× slower than sequential M=1**. The byte side says the same: at M=6 each layer reads 36 × 18.8 MB ≈ 677 MB, 40 layers ≈ **27 GB/batch** ≈ 3 s | Scheme A is only a correctness baseline, **not a performance path**. DSpark's TPS prediction should use small k (1–2), not k=5 |
| **34b** | **how much miss the union of a verify batch can save (F1 measurement, `tools/route_union.py`)** | **u(6) = 21.6** (not the extrapolated 26), and the miss the union saves relative to a hot cache is **0** | 34's "conditional GO" now hangs on a number **measured as 0**. **go/no-go is no longer an unknown**: as long as decode is I/O-bound, speculation loses up front. The only thing that can turn it around is "making decode no longer I/O-bound", which is a capacity/hit-rate matter. Also noting what was **never measured**: `GpuMoeBridge::record_batch_union` has no caller and has therefore never been executed; `--spec` is not wired into the CLI; and the criterion "same prompt, temperature 0, output token-identical with the `--dspark` switch on or off" has never been run on the real model |
| **34** | **speculation on an NVMe-bound decode, overall** | l3's M=1 is 1.28–1.33 ms/layer → 40 layers ≈ **51 ms**, but the measured hot step is **81.5 ms**; the ~30 ms difference is MoE+head, and MoE fetches experts from NVMe on demand. **The MoE cost of verify does not amortise with M, it grows linearly with the union** -- the union of 6 tokens is ≈26 experts/layer. The C(M) curve itself **passes** (M=6 worst 2.98×, criterion ≤3.06×), but MoE(M) does not. Overall: **conditional GO ×1.18–1.19** (confidence θ=0.5), just past the 1.15 threshold; fixed k does not pass (best ×1.14); and if the head degrades into an M=1 loop (`T_draft` 42 ms) it drops to **×1.11 = NO-GO** | `speculative` in `Engine::generate` is still `unimplemented`. Three kernel capabilities are missing (`p4_dspark_runtime.md` §2). **Speculative decoding only makes sense on the "weight read" side (the 3.65× of §2.3), but its MoE union eats that gain right back** -- this is the gate P5 has to close first |

### 3.6 Other things recorded

- **The `XLayout` enum never took effect**: `run_batch` never reads `layout`, and `column_x` fills all 6 columns in both modes, so `Staged ≡ OneColumn`. **Deleted**. The real bug underneath it: the host copied every column out of **column 0 of y**, while the weights are staged per column -- column 0 "happened" to be bit for bit correct, while columns 1/2 were off by 15–22% in |y|max. Fingerprint: the dispersion of `per-column weights only` was **0** before the fix and **5.235e+00** after
- **bf16 residual stream experiment** (would it put the 6th/7th expert back into the reference's order) -- design §15 issue 3, **never done**. The near-tie at layer 2: incoming cos 0.9997, MoE output **0.9883866**, gate 5/6
- **softplus in `gate.slang`**: `log(1+exp(z))` goes to zero below z≈−16, max |gpu−cpu| **1.5e-4**, affecting only experts with scores <1e-3. A one-line fix, **still open** (design §15 issue 20)
- ~~**`KvStore::clear()`** writes 163,840 `−inf` values element by element into the path A mapping on every `reset_context` -- a violation of §7.1 rule 10, **unfixed** (design §15 issue 26)~~ **Stale, corrected 2026-10-01: already fixed.** `runtime/kvstore.cpp` builds the `−inf` fill **once** in ordinary host memory (`ninf_`) and `memcpy`s it in, and its comment cites rule 10 and prices the scalar-loop version it replaced (16,384 uncached stores, ~230 ns each = 3.8 ms per `clear()`).
- **storing the compressed KV as bf16 instead of packed FP4**: saves 18 MB at 64K (66 vs 48 MB), at the cost of one nibble unpack in the `sparse_attn` inner loop. **Deliberately deferred**
- **P2 step 2's irreproducibility was not the kernel** -- it was **three tracks concurrently rebuilding `.spv` into the same `build/shaders` directory**. Evidence: step 2 reported a step-0 margin of **7.0927**, an isolated rebuild of its own commit gives **6.7477**, and with Track F's kernel it gives **6.9389** -- the runs that were cited **executed neither set of kernels**. **This is where the "quiet machine" rule comes from** (`plan_p5.md` §1)
- ~~**the A/B for `MOE_OVERLAP` / `PREFILL_HANDOFF` / `BACKFILL`**: the table was designed but **never run** (the sandbox gave `SetNamedSecurityInfoW failed (Win32 5)`). `p4_hitrate.md` §4 is still empty. **Not measured**~~ **Stale, corrected 2026-10-01.** That was the Windows attempt; **F4 later ran it** and the result is in `p4_hitrate.md` §6 and in §7 item 7 (`MOE_OVERLAP` +1-4% and on by default, `PREFILL_HANDOFF` +72% / +49% / +17% at 512 / 1,024 / 2,048 tokens and on by default, `BACKFILL` worth +0.0011 hit for 21.5 GiB more read and **off** by default). This line is what made the owner start re-running it on 2026-10-01; the run was killed once §7 item 7 was read. **Closed -- do not measure it again.**

### 3.7 The negative results of P4's four wrap-up tracks (2026-09-18) + Track Q1 (43, 44)

| # | Experiment | Measured | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **35** | **predictive prefetch** (Markov / token-id table / within-token co-occurrence / hidden-state lookahead d=1..8), on real routing traces, an honest disk model, 80-odd configurations × 253 full replays | At 4,500 / 5,711 slots **all of them are net negative or flat**, the best one +0.8%. Turning it positive requires **precision = 1.00**: at precision 0.9 it is still **15% slower** than not prefetching (5.47 vs 6.45 @ 5,711). "The last four layers" are not special (per-layer LRU hit 0.856–0.918 is nearly flat, layers 36–39 are 0.884–0.889; omnisciently prefetching only the last 4 layers is worth just +5.5%). Pinning whole layers is **uniformly worse** (pinning the last 4 layers −14%), a per-layer static hot set −0.5%, per-layer quotas −3.9%, and streaming whole layers needs 90–361 GB/s. Prediction-aware eviction ±0.1% | **Do not implement** (`p4_cache_policy.md` §9.1). Root cause: demand misses alone already need 95–106 ms of disk time per token, while the compute window is only 80 ms -- **the disk is already 25 ms in debt, so a wasted budget is negative**. The ceiling really is high (omniscient prefetch +50%, with omniscient eviction +101%), it is just **out of reach** |
| **36** | **a second NVMe (striped to 9 GB/s) vs any software policy** | **+32%** at C=5,711, +40% at C=4,500, with no prediction and no risk | **Not a negative result, a ranking**: it is bigger than every software policy this project has measured. design §3.1 already reserves room for the stripe. ~~The only software candidate in the same table that was never run is score-aware eviction (refreshing heat with the raw top-16 scores) -- the Belady ceiling is +42% and it is the one most likely to capture some of it, still unmeasured~~ **It was measured later (Track E1, the "the eviction-policy lever" row in §1, and 47 in §3): the best of 127 configurations was +2.3%, halved +1.2%, below the jitter band; the top-16 scores contribute only 0.06 points. Turned off.** |
| **37** | **2-bit routed expert** | 13 schemes. L3 end-to-end **PPL 29 → 14,290,394, argmax agrees 0 times over 64 positions**; the gap between the best and the worst scheme is **smaller than either one's distance from "usable"**. **3-bit does not pass either** (PPL 13.25M, top-1 1/64). The root cause is structural: the checkpoint is QAT'd to FP4, the code stream's entropy is **3.8375 bit** against the log2(15)=3.9069 ceiling, and the error is **spread perfectly evenly** across rows / blocks / matrices / layers / experts -- **there is no redundancy to squeeze and no significant subset to protect**. On the criterion side: **a relative expert-output error of 0.31 at L1 is already fatal end to end** | **NO-GO, at both bit widths** (`p4_quant2.md` §8). It used to be the biggest single number in this project's decode work (7.09 → 15.83 tok/s, ×2.23). **Redirected**: **about half** of that ×2.23 was really "the extra slots" and not "narrower reads" -- keeping FP4's hit and only halving the bytes gives 10.58 tok/s, and the slots can be had without touching a single weight. design §6's "use the checkpoint's own precision, do not change one bit" was **challenged seriously for the first time, and survived** |
| **38** | **resident-only routing (`all`) as the default**: route only to experts already in cache | 64-step teacher-forced L3: PPL **×1.82 – ×2.29** (criteria ×1.05 / ×1.30, three independent processes), gate mass lost **0.3054**, **52 / 2,560 layer-steps left with only the shared expert**, top-1 agreement with the reference 42/64 (`off` is 61/64). The speed really is ×2.74, and the output is **incoherent** | **NO-GO** (`p4_resident_routing.md` §9.4). The reason is the hard budget in section 3: background backfill enqueued 2,687 / **dropped 2,072 as expired** |
| **39** | **resident-only routing (`stall1`, downgrade one expert only when it would stall) as the default** | Quality **×1.09 – ×1.11** (mass lost 0.076, **0 layers left with only the shared expert**, top-1 51/64), speed ×1.21 on the harness but only ×1.07 over a four-turn conversation, at a cost of **1,624 P0s / 8.9 s of waiting** | **NO-GO as the default** (quality ×1.09 is over the ×1.05 line, and the speed does not buy it back). ~~**But its quality lands inside the ≤ ×1.3 band, so the premise for "use it only on DSpark's verify pass" holds**~~ --
**this sentence is overturned by 40**: `stall1`'s 0.076 was bought by **paying one P0 at every step**, while verify-only pays none at any of the four draft positions. It also overturns step 3's verdict (read at the time as ×3.40, worse even than `all`); the whole difference was in the ruler (§5.5) |
| **40** | **verify-only resident-only routing (`DEEPMOE_ROUTE_RESIDENT_ONLY=verify`) as "the default when speculation is on"**: within block-5, position 1 routes exactly and the 4 draft positions route only to already-resident experts, so the verify pass fetches not one byte from disk for them | 64-step teacher-forced L3: PPL **×1.376** (criteria ×1.05 / ×1.30), gate mass lost **0.2099**, **17 layer-steps left with only the shared expert**, top-1 49/64. The speed side is real: four-turn conversation **×1.57** (4.82 → 7.57 tok/s), **P0 bytes 262.5 → 62.5 GiB (−76%)**, total bytes −19%, and the output is still coherent. The intermediate setting in the other direction (the 4 draft positions switched to `stall1`) brings quality back to **×1.033 / mass lost 0.0649 / 0 shared-only**, but **only ×1.06 of the speed is left**, at a cost of 7,687 P0s / **42.9 s** of waiting | **NO-GO** (`p4_resident_routing.md` §10). It **overturns that premise sentence in 39** and the reading of §7 item 3: `stall1`'s quality was bought with one P0 per step, and dropping P0's opportunities from 5/5 to 1/5 drops quality from ×1.09 to ×1.376. The setting that can buy the quality back **makes the verify pass wait on the disk again**, which cancels the whole point of the scheme. **Note the basis**: speculative decoding does not exist in the engine (`Engine::forward_batch` is unwritten, `generate(speculative)` is `unimplemented`, `--spec` is not wired into the CLI), so what was measured is the verify pass's equivalent at M = 1, and that equivalent is **optimistically biased** (positions 2–5 of a real union batch use the cache state from before the fetch) |
| **41** | **bit-exact agreement between `Engine::forward_batch` and M=1 decode (M1's gate)** | Establish the ruler first: rerunning M=1 on the same reseeded state is **64/64 bit for bit identical**. Then the two paths: the first three layers **cos = 1.000000000 (bit for bit identical)**, L03 diverges by **3e-9**, L07 has the first **gate flip**, L39 cos 0.962. Over 64 steps (60 positions, block 5, warm cache, routing off) the worst cos is **0.9398**, top-1 **54/60**, max\|dlogit\| 8.26. With block = 1 the worst cos is 0.9928 -- **so this is not the batch boundary, it is the kernel-family difference amplified by near-ties in the gate**. Quality is unaffected: teacher-forced PPL **1.8589 vs M=1's 1.8857 (0.986×)**, reference 1.8177 | **Gate NO, implementation GO**. `docs/p4_dspark_runtime.md` §7.2. Direct consequence: design §10.2's hard invariant (temperature 0, token-identical output with speculation on or off) **cannot be met with this verify forward**. It also half-closes gap 4 (G3) of `p4_mgt1.md` §7: the mechanism has been isolated. **The ctest basis**: `suite.spec_forward` does not fail on this gate -- it prints cos / top-1 as a **WARN** and passes, failing only below a **regression floor** (cos < 0.93 or top-1 < 50/60; measured 0.9398 / 54/60); only `DEEPMOE_SPEC_STRICT=1` asserts the original condition |
| **43** | **"P0 gets the disk to itself" (non-P0 yields) as a speedup** | Instrumentation measured that in decode **0.0%** of P0s have any non-P0 chunk in flight when they are issued (even with backfill on it is only 8.2%, averaging 1.02 chunks = the one from `kBackgroundOpsWhileBusy`). `DEEPMOE_IO_BG_CAP_BUSY=0` (background **stops completely** while P0 is active): 4.846 → **4.830 tok/s (−0.3%)**, stall 105.8 → 105.4. Pulling engram (P2) into the throttling as well (`DEEPMOE_IO_BG_THROTTLE_P2=1`) **goes the other way**: **4.846 → 3.500 (−27.8%)**, and the breakdown points cleanly at the cause -- `nvme_stall` is unchanged (105.8 → 103.9) while **`engram` goes 4.5 → 85.8 ms/token** | **NO-GO** (`p4_p0_queue.md` §3). The prediction was "stall 111 → 75, tok/s +25%", and the **measurement was 105.2 → 105.4, −0.3%**: whatever road could be yielded, today's throttling yielded long ago. Plus one **permanent "do not do"**: **never throttle P2 engram**, its 264 B row reads are on the critical path |
| **44** | **a deeper queue / bigger chunks for P0** | `DEEPMOE_IO_P0_QD=32` + 256 MiB in flight: average QD at issue 3.80 → 6.04, but **each P0 is slower** (6.70 → 7.70 ms), tok/s +0.6% (jitter band ±3%, four measured baselines within ±0.5%). `P0_CHUNK_MB=16` (one op per run, exactly the fastest shape in `nvme_bench`): **−8.4%**, stall +17%. In the other direction: 2 MiB +0.9%, **1 MiB +1.4%** (P0 latency 6.70 → 5.76, the first one of a layer 2.83 → **1.30 ms**) | **None of them become the default** (`p4_p0_queue.md` §3, §6). The 1 MiB direction is consistent -- three B cells against four A cells are all positive -- but **+1.4% < the ±3% criterion band**. The real finding is in §4: **the disk is flat with respect to request size** (1 MiB–18.4 MiB, all 5.07–5.16 GB/s at QD ≥ 4), **while the engine is not** -- so that 30% gap is on the engine side (the destination being GPU-visible memory, or the dispatcher's refill rate), **not on the disk and not in the queueing**. The knobs (five `DEEPMOE_IO_*`) stay, with defaults equal to today's |

Measured unions u(2..6) = 9.90/13.35/16.53/19.35/22.13, within **≤ 2%** of `route_union.py`'s offline values. Acceptance length (`tools/spec_longest.py`, 71 greedy events): `chain` E[tokens](k=5) **3.82**, `longest` **4.77**, off-chain top-16 hit **0.5419**. With **drafts completely free**, per emitted token: `chain` k=5 **0.86× (slower)**, k=1 1.06–1.13×; `longest` k=5 1.06–1.07×. `T_draft` 19–42 ms/cycle = +4…+23 ms per token, **all flat or worse** | **NO-GO, for the third time, and this time from engine measurements**. And this is still the **best case, compute-bound** (P0 = 0); under NVMe-bound conditions the [1.00×, 1.80×] miss interval of §6.5 only makes it worse. `longest` is also **not lossless**: verify's row i+1 is conditioned on the chain's token, not on the one substituted in |
| **45** | **"the destination is GPU-visible memory, so the DMA is slow"** (candidate i left over from Q1 §6), and staging for it (read into pinned host memory, then copy into path A) | `bench/io_dst_bench`: the same `IoEngine`, the same chunk, the same shard on D:, **only the destination changes**. Throughput at QD 8: `ram` 4.722 / `pinned` 4.758 / **`patha` 4.511** / `pathb` 4.818 GB/s -- **path A is only 5% slower, not 30%**. What really is 8–10× apart is `Backend::submit`: **704 µs vs 70–122 µs**. The measured cost of staging: a `memcpy` of 18.8 MB takes **0.88 ms (21.5 GB/s)**, and 21.3 misses per token = **18.7 ms**, more expensive than the ~8 ms it was meant to save. "path B first" is likewise not done: after parallelising submission, path A's 4.21 → **4.80** has already caught up with path B's 4.82 | **Candidate i holds but was read in the wrong direction; staging / path-B-first are NO-GO** (`p4_p0_queue.md` §9, §12.2). The destination memory **is** the cause, but its cost is in **submission** (page locking) and not in the transfer, so the right fix is to parallelise submission rather than change the memory |

The "1 MiB chunk +1.4%" in Q1 §3 also changes explanation -- it is not that the disk likes small requests (D: is slower for 1 MiB), it is that a smaller chunk lets that **single-threaded submission path** start the next one sooner |
| **48** | **the viability of persistent-dispatch decode (plan_p5 §3(a))**: `build/residency_probe`, 8060S / Vulkan | Three parts. **The residency ceiling is ~406 256-thread workgroups** (32/64/128/256 all live; from 512 up the peak sticks at 406–413). **Cross-workgroup handshake: out of 1,000 round trips the 3rd one already TIMEOUTs** -- two workgroups (far below the 406 ceiling) cannot be guaranteed to make progress at the same time, so fleet's 1.36 µs reference value **simply cannot be measured here**. Forward progress: 2 / 8 / 32 / 128 groups `progressed`, 512 / 2048 groups `TIMEOUT` (over the ceiling, as expected) | **NO-GO, closed like (b)** (`bench/results/p4pd/residency_probe.csv`). Criterion 2 of plan_p5 §5.1's three fails outright: **spin-waiting is unusable on this machine**, so the device-side task queue + event counter shape does not hold. (a) therefore reduces to "one dispatch graph", and 49 shows that graph is worth **0.8 ms/token**. Cost: ten minutes |

And the **distribution of those 16.84 ms is not flat**: **all 16.04 ms sit in front of each layer's MoE dispatch** (40 × 0.40 ms, the host round trip where the command buffer is cut at the gate), while attention / ced / tail's **700 dispatches together come to only 0.80 ms** (in-layer barriers are 0.4–2.2 µs each, consistent with the 0.56–0.66 µs of 27 in §3). Per layer: a normal layer has 17 dispatches, 1,895 µs busy, in-layer gap **18 µs** + 335 µs before MoE; a source layer has 26 dispatches, 2,112 µs busy, in-layer gap **24 µs** + 271 µs before MoE | **c1–c3 struck out, and the cheap form of (a) is already in the tree**. "Record a layer's non-MoE chain into one command buffer" **is what is implemented today** (§2.1, ~128 → 41 submits, cut only at the gate); the ceiling for fusing any further dispatches is **0.80 ms/token = 0.8%**, a quarter of one ±3% jitter band. The remaining 16.0 ms has only one mechanism that can reach it -- a device-side gate -- **and 48 closed that**. The trace itself is **free**: 102.07 ms with `--trace` vs 102.3–106.2 ms without |

DISJOINT (rawread's slicing) **collapses on the same row from 16 readers on**: 4 MiB 91.4 / 94.3 / 93.8 / 91.6 / **59.0 / 29.7 / 20.5 / 10.2 / 5.1** -- aggregate bandwidth is capped and each reader is thinned linearly | **Sharing is free (up to ~16–32 workgroups), so "read once then publish" cannot possibly pay** (`bench/results/p4pd/sharing_probe.csv`). This explains 10 (`heads_per_wg` 1/2/4/8 = 69/100/169/288 µs) and 11 (`pv_heads_per_wg=8` 120 µs vs 25) in §3 in one go: they were not slow because of repeated reads, repeated reads were never charged for. **It closes off every future proposal of the same shape** |
| **47** | **score-aware eviction** ((a) of §7 item 1, the project's "only eviction policy never run"), together with ARC / LRU-K / LFU-decay / S3-FIFO / TinyLFU-style admission, all put through Track X's time model (`tools/cache_evict_study.py`, 4.6 GB/s, demand-only, 127 configurations) | The best implementable shape is **`rank = last_use + α × heat`** (α ≈ 1,600–4,800, the ridge is very flat): on the full trace **hit 0.9073 → 0.9120 @ C=5,100**, **+2.3% on the test set** (+2.7% at 4,500, +1.8% at 5,711). **Turning the raw top-16 scores off entirely costs only 0.06 pt**. `cache_sim`'s `score-aware` as-is (sorting by heat) gives **hit 0.5345 = −68%**; LRU-2 / LFU-decay **−68%**; ARC **−1.5%**; refusing admission by score −0.1%…−33%. Belady on the same basis is **+40%** | **NO-GO** (`p4_cache_policy.md` §12). Halved it is **+1.2%**, below `p4_p0_queue.md` §3's **±3% jitter band** -- even written it could not be measured. **The falsification condition of §9.4 ablation #3 was measured and hit**: the top-16 scores carry no usable information, and all 40% of Belady's is in "the future". The mechanism: fine-grained LRU **already is** an "earliest possible next use" ordering (an expert in layer L has to wait at least 40 layer-steps, the same constant offset for every key), so the periodic structure holds no free information. `store/planner.cpp`'s `ScoreAwarePolicy` stays as a fallback to LRU, and that TODO becomes "do not do" |

The **ceiling** for a `provisional gate` (predicting L+1 from layer L's pre-MoE residual, buying two layers of lead) is **+3.5%** | **NO-GO** (`p4_idle_prefetch.md` §5). Halved it is **+1.8%**, below the ±3% jitter band. **The acceptance line is from now on a single number**: per-expert precision **≥ 0.77** at two layers of lead (at p=0.77 it is +9.9% raw / +5.0% halved), while the most optimistic reading (the true post-MoE gate) is only **0.674**. Also correcting an off-by-one along the way: `route_trace.py`'s `snapshot[L]` is taken **after** `run_layer(L)`, so what is available at the start of layer L is `pred_d(d+1)` and not `pred_dd` -- getting that one index wrong gives away a free layer of lead and a whole precision notch |
| **52** | **"the MoE / attention kernels still have bandwidth headroom"** (Track F6's roofline, all computed from existing data, no new measurements) | MoE: 4.512 GB/token ÷ `moe_gpu` 42.14 ms = **107 GB/s = 50% of UMA(216)**, which looks like 16 ms available; but `kernel_p2_moe.md` §3.5's best M=1 variant is **222.6 GB/s = 102% of the ceiling** (0.5912 ms per dispatch pair, 7 FP4 slots) -- **the kernel is already saturated**, `MoeRunner`'s 1.044 ms per layer is nearly equal to the engine's 1.053, and **the whole 18.5–21.9 ms/token gap is in dispatch / gaps** (host-side `submit` is only 3.73 ms over 56.5 calls). attention/dense: summing tensor by tensor gives **8,522.8 MB/token = byte for byte equal to the engine's `hot_bytes` 8,522,849,728**, ÷ `attn` 43.51 ms = **196 GB/s = 91% of UMA**, and the kernel floor (`attn_bench` P3 33.5 ms + shared expert 6.53 ms) is **40.0 ms** | **The attention line is closed** (`p4_idle_prefetch.md` §8): it is already above 80%, "lifting it to 80%" is **5.8 ms slower**, and saturating it to 100% is worth only **+1.1% (halved)**. **That ≥5% on MoE is real, but it is not in the shader**: pressing it down to the kernel's own rate is **+5.2%…+6.3% (halved)**, and that money is booked under §7 item 4 (the per-dispatch trace) |
| **53** | **"shrink the gate's host round trip itself"** (Track G, `plan_p5.md` §3(g)) -- the last road to the 16.0 ms that 49 left behind. Two new instruments: `DEEPMOE_GATE_PROBE=1` (host-side timing per segment, hits and misses on separate lines) and `tools/trace_timeline.py --gate` (GPU-side, printing the pre-MoE gap per layer; it reproduced PD's 102.07 / 16.04 / 0.80 on PD's own trace) | Hot step, fully resident, 40 layer-steps, **µs/layer**: GPU-side gap **386.1** = **220.9** after the host-side fence + **165.2** that cannot be measured (fence wakeup + submit → GPU start). The breakdown of the 220.9: **the single `vkQueueSubmit2` call 99.5**, MoE staging 44.7, **the next layer's bind + record_attention 32.6** (the submit that carries layer L's MoE happens inside `run_layer(L+1)`, so L+1's prologue sits in L's idle window), `verify` + reading top-16 **26.0**, recording MoE 9.7, planner 8.1, planner wait 0.35. **(d) does not exist**: the gate kernel already writes top-16 into host-coherent memory, so there is no readback to save. Attacking (a) landed as `DEEPMOE_FENCE_SPIN_US` (poll the timeline counter, fall back to blocking past the budget): gap **386.1 → 325.2 (−60.9)**, **of which only ≈15 µs is wakeup latency, while 45.4 µs is the whole host round trip getting faster after the wakeup** (the same `vkQueueSubmit2` 99.5 → 76.0 µs, the same `vkCmd*` stretch 9.7 → 4.1) -- **the cost of parking is mostly not the wakeup, it is what comes after the wakeup**.
